  2. In digestion: the last matter of food, out of which the first Ens of the body’s nourishment arises by the Archaeus. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “The first Ens or Being toward life, from whence the body hath it’s nourishment & food ariseth out of the last matter of the Meat by the Archaeus” [CH5.33]

Lesser world

The book spells it: lesser World, lesser world

  1. Man, as the small world that agrees with the great world, one with it in essence and inward form though not in outward form (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the agreement of the lesser World Man with the greater and externall world” [CH1.4]
    • “as is the great world, so is the lesser, in essence and internall form they are altogether one and the same thing” [CH1.5]
    • “the superiour and inferiour things of the greater and lesser world” [MICRO.45]
  2. a likeness for gathering into one: spirituall virtues and active qualities are heaped up together by Art into one masse, like the lesser world (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “being by great help of ingenuity and Art like the lesser world heaped up together and concentred into one masse” [CH6.49]

Light of Grace

The book spells it: Light of Grace, light of Grace, Light of GRACE

  1. One of the two Lights, named with Theology and set beside the Light of Nature: it begets a true theologer, physick rises from it, and by it come eternal life and the intimate vision of God. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “It taketh its rise from Theology or the Light of Grace, and endeth in the Light of Nature.” [CH2.28]
    • “The Light of Grace, begetteth a true Theologer, yet not without Phylosophy” [CH5.19]
    • “The two Lights are well known, within which are all thing, without which is nothing” [CH5.19]
    • “by the supernall help of the Light of Grace we may together with Christ and all the Elect possesse that Eternall Life” [CH5.13]
    • “It is the intimate vision of GOD, and the Intuitive knowledge of GOD, which also hapneth by the Light of Grace to the separate Soul even in this world” [CH6.68]
    • “which Treasures are to be manifest indifferently to all in due time by the light of Grace and Nature” [OPEN.10]
    • “make manifest the Light of GRACE and NATURE” [CH6.76]

Light of Nature

The book spells it: Light of Nature, light of Nature

  1. A divine analogy of the visible world with the body of man. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Light of Nature, which is nothing else but a divine Analogy of this visible world with the body of man” [CH1.5]
    • “What the Light of Nature is.” [N039]
  2. The firmament, or the great world, which supplies man with all things. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “for the Firmament is the Light of Nature, which naturally supplyeth man with all things” [CH1.41]
    • “Without the knowledge of Light of Nature or the great world” [N040]
  3. An inward teacher in man, named beside the inward invisible Man and the internal Angel, of which the physician is to be born. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the inward and invisible Man, the internall Angell, the Light of Nature, which like a sound Doctour teacheth and instructeth men” [CH1.1]
    • “A Physitian should be born out of the Light of Grace and Nature” [CH1.1]
    • “a good Physitian, born of the Light of Nature” [CH5.12]
  4. Man’s own power to discern and judge, which no one may take from another and which no single author holds. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the liberty of human ingenuity, that Light of Nature, the power to discern and judge as well as himselfe” [CH5.1]
    • “nor was the whole Light of Nature drawne into Galen and limited in him only” [CH5.1]
  5. One of the two Lights, paired with the Light of Grace: the Treasury of God, which makes a true philosopher, as the Light of Grace makes a theologian. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “The Light of Nature, which is the Treasury of God confirmed in the Scriptures, maketh a true Phylosopher” [CH5.19]
    • “The Light of Grace, begetteth a true Theologer” [CH5.19]
    • “It taketh its rise from Theology or the Light of Grace, and endeth in the Light of Nature.” [CH2.28]
    • “by the light of Grace and Nature” [OPEN.10]
    • “make manifest the Light of GRACE and NATURE” [CH6.76]
    • “God is known when the Light of Nature” [N086]
  6. A source of knowledge that shows things and by which men become skilled and philosophize. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Light of Nature doth manifestly shew” [MICRO.40]
    • “all that truly professed Phylosophy from the Light of Nature” [MICRO.21]
    • “who by the light of Nature shall be skillfull in the Chymicks” [OPEN.10]

Limbus

The book spells it: Limbus

  1. Paracelsus’ name for the First Matter, the seed of all things to be created, whose Earth was the Word of the Lord. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “So the First Matter (which Paracelsus calleth the Limbus, whose Earth was the WORD of the Lord) was the seed of all things that were to be created” [MICRO.17]
  2. The greater world, from which man’s first spirit comes. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “The first Spirit is from the Limbus or greater world; the second from the word Fiat.” [N101]
  3. The body of the Limbus, to be joined with the spiracle, called the Spirit of life, as in one marriage. (veiled · Stated · voice: UNCLEAR · note-only)
    • “The Spirit of life is the Spiracle or breathing hole.” [N122]
    • “The body of the Limbus & spiracle ought to make one Marriage” [N122]

Limus Terrae

The book spells it: Limus Terrae, Limo Terrae, LIMO TERRAE, limo terrae

  1. The slime, clay or dust of the earth out of which man was taken, which the text explains as the frame of the whole world, the great world, man’s mortal father; it is two-fold, visible and invisible, and called a hidden secret thing. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “drawn or taken (è limo terrae) out of the slime of the Earth or visible frame of the whole consisting of Heaven or Heavenly Sphaeres and the Earth” [MICRO.11]
    • “Man was created è Limo Terrae, out of the clay or dust of the Earth” [MICRO.22]
    • “ex LIMO TERRAE, out of the dust of the ground or great world as to the mortall Body which is visible and invisible” [MICRO.31]
    • “a Mortall one, which is the whole world with all the creatures, that is, that Limus Terrae, that slime of the earth, or hidden Secret thing” [MICRO.17]
    • “The Limus Terrae, or dust of the Earth, is two-fold, visible and invisible” [MICRO.23]
  2. Man himself, named with the Microcosm, as made up of all bodies and created things. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Microcosm or Limus Terrae, Man is compacted of all bodies and created things” [MICRO.12]
  3. The earthly mind, set against the image or inspiration of the most High, after which the friends of God do not live. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “who live after the Image or inspiration of the most High, and not after the Limus Terrae the Eearthy Mind” [CH6.68]

Little world

The book spells it: little world, little World

  1. Man, and his body, as the small copy of the great world: one and the same with it in all things, preserved, nourished and cured by it. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the little World Man” [OPEN.24]
    • “For that little world Man is one and the same in all things with his-Parant, the great world” [MICRO.31]
    • “Man is therefore called a Microcosm or little world, because the whole world preserveth, nourisheth and cureth him” [CH4.6]
    • “in the little world, the body, there lurketh perpetuall strife” [CH3.6]
    • “by destroying the frame of the little world and taking away our life” [CH5.44]
    • “the Harmony between the great and little world” [CH5.46]

Lodestone

The book spells it: Load-stone, Load stone, Loadstone

  1. The drawing stone itself, used in plaisters and named as a likeness for things that attract or work invisibly. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “by application of drawing Plaisters made of the Load-stone and other things” [CH4.13]
    • “As the Earthy Load stone in its body is a spirit and draweth to it selfe” [MICRO.34]
    • “The Imagination or Fantasie of Man is like the Load stone in its Nature, attracting the Fantasie of other men” [MICRO.40]
    • “those wonderfull vertues in the Nature of the Antipathicall Loadstone, which doth (as it were) bewitch spiritually and invisibly” [CH6.73]
  2. The sydereall spirit in man, born of the Astra’s and united to them, which is called the Load stone with a magnetick nature in man; the body and spirit in man that draw are called the Load stone of the Microcosm, and attract the force of the Astra’s. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “This sydereall Spirit which is born of the Astra’s together with Man (and therefore remains united to the Astra’s) is the Load stone and hath a magnetick nature in man.” [MICRO.34]
    • “so also the body and spirit in Man doe draw unto him by a visible body, this is the Load stone of the Microcosm” [MICRO.34]
    • “The sydereall body and spirit attract unto them the force of the Astra’s” [MICRO.34]

Macrocosm

The book spells it: Macrocosm

  1. The great world, set over against man the little world as parent to child; its parts answer man’s parts, and its like fruits, Salt, Sulphur and Mercury restore and preserve his. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Microcosm the child ought always to answer to the fourfold members of the Macrocosm its parent” [CH1.12]
    • “they must be restored by fruits like themselves of the Macrocosm” [CH4.6]
    • “the Salt, Sulphur and Mercury of the Microcosm is preserved by the Salt, Sulphur, and Mercury of the Macrocosm analogically agreeing together” [CH4.7]
    • “When the Phylosopher comes to a stand in the Naturall Light of the Macrocosm, then the Physitian begins to move and proceed in the Analogicall Concordance of the Naturall Light of the Microcosm with that of the great world.” [CH5.11]

Magic

The book spells it: Magick

  1. True and lawful Magick, set apart from witchcraft: the perfection of philosophy and full knowledge of hidden Nature, which does things beyond Nature’s usual order by applying Agents and Patients according to the consent and dissent of things; far better treasures lie in it, with the true Cabala and Woarchadumie. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “every were I mean that which is True and lawfull” [CH5.55]
    • “or wichcraft, which is fit for none but Vulcan, and deserveth to be burned” [CH5.55]
    • “the absolute perfection of the whole most noble Phylosophy, the chiefest Wisdome of Gods works and the plenary knowledge of occult Nature” [CH5.55]
    • “doth things exceeding the usuall order of Nature, by a fit application of Agents and Patients, observing the consent and dissent of things” [CH5.55]
    • “in the true Cabala, Magick and Woarchadumie, there are laid up far better Treasures” [OPEN.10]
  2. Naturall Magick and its kinds: Gamahaea, which does by Art invisibly what Nature does visibly, and the Gabalisticall part, which works through the Imagination; the margin sets Magick beside Faith transplanting minds. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “This fourth kind of Naturall Magick called Gamahaea doth all things invisibly and spiritually, by the help of Art, which Nature is able to doe visibly and corporally without such help.” [MICRO.35]
    • “This part of Magick is called Gabalisticall, and is supported with three Pillars.” [MICRO.37]
    • “Hence the true Magician or wise Man can attract the operation of the Astra’s, stones, mettalls, &c. into the Imagination” [MICRO.37]
    • “Magick or Faith transplanting minds hath power over all Spirits and Ascendants.” [N128]
    • “thus by reall Magick he shewed to that great Picus the hidden virtue or power of Man” [N124]
  3. Interpreted: an incomprehensible working by which what God planted in the Elements at the Creation shall ever be supplied out of the Eternall Treasures of Divine Wisdome. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Severinus · wording-dependent)
    • “shall ever be supplyed by an incomprehensible Magick out of the Eternall Treasures of Divine Wisdome” [CH1.23]
  4. Mechanick Magick: by its help, the author says, the Element of Earth should swim upon the water, a thing the vulgar sort can hardly believe. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “that the Element of EARTH should by the help of Mechanick Magick swim upon the water” [CH6.40]

Magician

The book spells it: Magician

  1. The true Magician, glossed as the wise man: one who alone can discern physic as a spirit, can draw the operation of the stars, stones and metals into the imagination, and is not below the angels in magical operation. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Physick is a Spirit (not a body) which the Magician or wise man only can discerne” [CH4.6]
    • “the true Magician or wise Man can attract the operation of the Astra’s, stones, mettalls, &c. into the Imagination” [MICRO.37]
    • “And if he be a true Magician, he is not inferiour to the Angells in all Magicall operation” [MICRO.24]

Magistery

The book spells it: magistery, Magistery, Magisteryes

  1. Mastership: Nature’s own, in which it makes itself merry (margin note only). (plain · Stated · voice: UNCLEAR)
    • “Nature many times makes it selfe merry with its magistery or mastership and rejoyceth in its master, and in its art and vertues” [N178]
  2. The philosophers’ work itself, into which Lully searched and whose origin a margin note heads; here tied to the matter of the Elixer. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “Raymund Lully that fearcher into this Magistery” [CH3.36]
    • “the matter of the Elixer” [CH3.36]
    • “The originall of the Philosophicall Magistery” [N278]
  3. The work of this Art, what is hidden in which God opens to the Artist. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Lullius)
    • “that he may open to him what is hid in the magistery of this Art” [CH6.44]
  4. A kind of prepared medicine, counted among the Arcana beside Fift Essences and named with Elixirs. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “Of this sort are the Fift Essences, Magisteryes, and the like” [CH1.43]
    • “to learn the preparations of those high Magisteryes and Elixirs” [CH3.40]

Matrix

The book spells it: matrix, Matrix, matrices

  1. A womb: the place in which a seed or Astrum dies and rots, and in which it brings forth its fruit; the Astrum of every Element brings forth its fruits in a strange matrix. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the four Astra’s of the said Elements are the Seeds in the four matrices or wombs” [CH1.35]
    • “so soon as ever the Seed or Astrum dies and rots in its matrix or womb, the Astrum goes forward into a new body” [CH1.35]
    • “For the Astrum of every Element brings forth and bayes its fruits in a strange region or matrix” [CH1.30]
    • “generating matrix, as the fish in the water” [N060]
  2. The mother out of which a whole is brought forth: Water is the Matrix of the world, the great world the matrix of Adam (margin notes only). (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Water is the Matrix of the world, upon which the Spirit of God moved” [N104]
    • “The great world was the matrix of Adam, thus the whole frame of the world is the mother of all things that are brought forth” [N118]
  3. A place of birth in which the three first are found, as in every birth from it. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Severinus)
    • “these three are found in every matrix” [CH1.25]

Matter

The book spells it: matter, Matter

  1. First matter, in which all things were created perfect, and last matter, which the Chymist perfects by Fire. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “ALL things in respect of the first matter were created perfect, but the Chymist perfecteth the last matter by Fire” [CH3.1]
  2. The one Nature as the matter of all things. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “that one onely Nature the Essence and Matter of all things” [CH3.30]
  3. The gross bodily matter in which a medicine’s power lies drowned and hindered, and from which secrets work apart. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “then whilst they are drowned in Matter, which alwayes hindereth and restraineth the power and operation of the Secret” [CH3.14]
    • “so far as they are yet drowned in a thick grosse matter” [CH6.58]
    • “Such is the Nature of all Secrets that they worke without the matter and body” [CH4.6]
  4. The pure matter of a medicine got by separation; and the most simple and incorruptible matter of the Fift Essence or Medicine, generated out of the spirit of this world, that is, the Soul or Spirit of the world. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “that that pure and Christiline matter may be administred to our bodies” [CH3.7]
    • “it is the most simple Matter which the Best and Greatest Lord generated out of the spirit of this world for the restauration and preservation of Humane Nature” [CH6.51]
    • “this Medicine is made of the more incorruptible and efficacious Matter that can be under Heaven, that is, the Soule or Spirit of the world” [CH6.55]
  5. The matter of the Phylosophers’ Mystery, to be had everywhere. (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: the Phylosophers)
    • “the Phylosophers say that the matter of their Mystery may be had every where, because it consisteth in every Elementated thing” [CH3.34]
  6. The matter of the Elixer, which Morienes, asked by King Calid, says is of the king himself. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Morienes)
    • “King Calid who inquired after the matter of the Elixer” [CH3.36]
    • “It is of thee O King, said he” [CH3.36]
  7. Lully’s matter, got out of a mean and worthless thing. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Raymund Lully)
    • “he saith that he got his matter out of a mean and worthlesse thing” [CH3.36]
  8. The matter of the stone, said to be in every thing in respect of the Vegetall Spirit. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “The matter of a stone is said to be in every thing” [N180]
    • “which is calld the Vegetall Spirit, by whom our matter of a stone goeth beyond the rest” [N180]
  9. The matter of diseases, which Paracelsus calls Tartar. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Paracels · note-only)
    • “Paracels saith that the Matter, viz. the Tartar of diseases is twofold” [N219]

Mean

The book spells it: mean, Mean

  1. A middle thing that shares in two extremes and joins them: the astral body, in which the intellectual soul and the earthly body are united into one man. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “wherein the Intellectuall Soul and earthy Body like two Extreams are knit, glued and confederate together” [MICRO.17]
    • “in this third mean which partaketh of the other two they are coupled and united into one intire man” [MICRO.17]
  2. Our Saviour as the Mean by which alone God and man are united, sharing both natures. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “Thus God and Man cannot be united but by a Mean, even our Saviour who partaketh of two Natures” [N109]
  3. The mean that fallen man turned from, to himself as the extreme. Several readings: (1) the middle place man was set in, between Time and Eternity, in the midst of the Paradise of this world (MICRO.10); (2) the unity man departed from, as a margin note puts the Fall as a going from Unity to Alterity (N212). (veiled · several · voice: CROLL)
    • “turning from the mean to (the extreame) himselfe” [MICRO.10]
    • “being set between Time and Eternity” [MICRO.10]
    • “that being set in the midst of the Paradice of this world” [MICRO.10]
    • “The Fall of Man and our great evill is to depart from Unity to Alterity.” [N212]

Medicine

The book spells it: Medicine, medicine, Medicines

  1. A remedy given for disease. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “who in the fear of God also shall use these Medicines rightly prepared by their own labour” [OPEN.8]
    • “Though sometimes it may be the best medicine not to use any medicine at all” [CH4.6]
  2. The one chief Medicine of the most ancient philosophers, made of the soul or spirit of the world and created by God for the preservation of life. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Of that one onely chiefe Medicine of the most Ancient Phylosophers.” [CH6.0]
    • “except only that universall and most famous Medicine which the most ancient wise Philosophers had from the beginning of the world” [OPEN.6]
    • “this Medicine is made of the more incorruptible and efficacious Matter that can be under Heaven, that is, the Soule or Spirit of the world” [CH6.55]
    • “this foresaid Medicine, which is in it selfe the matter of our Creation” [CH6.52]
    • “God hath created the aforesaid Medicine for the preservation of life” [CH6.71]

Meditation

The book spells it: meditation

  1. Frequent thinking by which the manner, cause and reason of each thing is found out; part of the study of nature. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “By this meditation, which is a frequent cogitation, the manner, cause & reason of every manner of thing is found out.” [N201]
    • “by which industry and that perpetuall thirst which they have of meditation and cogitation” [CH5.10]
  2. A quiet religious meditation by which the adepts are raised up out of the sepulchre of their body, so that they can be turned to God. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “by a quiet and religious meditation been raised up out of the sepulchre of their body” [CH6.68]

Mercury

The book spells it: Mercury

  1. one of the Three Principles of every compound body, with Sulphur and Salt: the liquor or moisture, which gives virtues and cherishes the other two, which grow dry and old (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Into Mercury or liquor, Sulphur or Oyle, and Salt” [CH1.19]
    • “Mercury, which like the Elixir giveth the vertues” [CH1.19]
    • “by a diligent and constant supply of the vital and vegetative moysture doth cherish the two former, which by frequent action continually grow dry and old” [CH1.19]
    • “found either in the Salt, or Mercury, or Sulphur of the same body” [CH3.8]
    • “As there are Three from which all Physick proceedeth, Mercury, Sulphur, Salt” [CH4.10]
    • “All Ulcers are cured by incarnating Mercury” [CH4.10]
    • “Mercury or the moisture of that which nourisheth” [CH5.33]
  2. quicksilver, to which the watery moisture of green wood is likened (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the first watry like fugitive Mercury or Quicksilver” [CH1.19]
  3. Mercury that, exalted above its natural degree in three ways, causes diseases (veiled · Interpreted · voice: UNCLEAR)
    • “Mercury may three manner of wayes be exalted above the Naturall degree” [CH4.8]
    • “By the heat of digestion it is distild, and so causeth all sorts of Apoplexies” [CH4.8]

Metals

The book spells it: mettalls, Mettalls, Mertalls

  1. Metals as fruit of the Elements, created not for man’s food but for his physick. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “although they are the fruit of Elements as well as living things and Vegetables, and created, though not for Mans food yet for his Physick” [CH3.28]
  2. The view, which the author answers, that metals are of no use in physick. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: any who out of their simplicity)
    • “that mettalls are of no use in Physick” [CH3.28]
  3. Perfect metals as the things in which, above all, God has written on Nature with his own finger. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “upon which God hath written with his own sacred finger in sublunary things, but especially in perfect Mettalls” [CH1.1]
  4. Metals as riches, left to those driven by the desire of being rich. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “leaving the mettalls to those who with an impious hunger, and a thirsty and insatiable desire of being rich” [CH6.11]
  5. Metals as things changed, their metamorphosis named beside the curing of diseases. (plain · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Phylosophers our predecessors that have been train’d up in Hermes school)
    • “in the curing of diseases and the metamorphosis of Mettalls” [CH6.50]
  6. Metals as the source of the colour and tincture of precious stones, which are metals transplanted. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “precious stones have the Colour, Forme and Tincture more or lesse from Mettalls by formation of the Stars” [CH3.27]
    • “for they are transplanted Mertalls” [CH3.27]

Method

The book spells it: Method, method

  1. True method: knowing the disease and curing it; the method, grounded in reason and experience, the author says Paracelsus kept. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “True Method consisteth in the knowledg of the Disease, and the Cure of it” [CH5.31]
    • “Paracelsus observed onely that method that was agreeable to Reason and Experience” [CH5.30]
  2. The right method the Medodists charge the Chymists with losing; reported in order to be rejected. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Medodists)
    • “falsly charge the genuine Chymists that they have lost the right method” [CH5.30]
  3. The Method of Physick that men who make a flourish of physick in words but do no cures boast they have; the author calls it a sign of foolish ambition. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “boasting that they have the Method of Physick” [CH5.29]
  4. An ordered way of preparing or curing, as the method of Hippocrates in curing diseases. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “but to observe the method of Hippocrates in curing diseases” [CH5.61]
    • “in a convenient and artificiall method” [OPEN.8]

Microcosm

The book spells it: Microcosm, MICROCOSM, Microcosme, microcosm

  1. Man as the little world, into whom the whole great world is gathered, answering to the Macrocosm as child to parent (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “OF THE MICROCOSM, Or Little World MAN” [MICRO.0]
    • “which few now a dayes understand, that the great visible World was made Man” [MICRO.14]
    • “Man is therefore called a Microcosm or little world, because the whole world preserveth, nourisheth and cureth him” [CH4.6]
    • “For the whole frame of the world is collected and reduced into the Microcosm” [MICRO.31]
    • “the Microcosm the child ought always to answer to the fourfold members of the Macrocosm its parent” [CH1.12]
    • “the Nature, as well of the Macrocosme as the Microcosme, is its own medicine, disease, and Physitian” [CH1.6]
    • “reduce the whole Microcosm into a true Temperament” [CH6.38]
    • “The Innate Heat and Radical Moysture of the Microcosm are sustained by the MacrocoMacrocosmicall Heat and Moysture of the Sun and Moon” [N293]
  2. the name Microcosm for Man, given by the Ancients (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Ancients)
    • “the prudent Ancients wilsely called Man a Microcosm or little World” [MICRO.14]
  3. Man as holding every particular of the Universe (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Interpreters of Secret Nature)
    • “there is nothing in the whole Universe, every particular whereof is not also in that Microcosm Man” [CH2.4]
  4. the word set beside the outer world: the externall Elements “are the very Microcosm” (CH1.6), and man’s Parent is called “the externall Heart of the Microcosm” (CH2.4). Readings: (1) the outer world or its Elements are themselves called Microcosm, because they are like man (basis: “for they are alike” (CH1.6); “his Parent or” (CH2.4)); (2) the word still names man, and the outer things are tied to him as his likeness: in CH1.6 “they” may be the Elements and Man together, in CH2.4 the externall Heart is the one that answers to man’s (basis: “Man also is understood” (CH1.6); “which is one like the other” (CH2.4)) (veiled · several · voice: CROLL · wording-dependent)
    • “all the externall Elements represent unto us the whole Man, which being known, Man also is understood, for they are alike, and are the very Microcosm” [CH1.6]
    • “his Parent or the externall Heart of the Microcosm which is one like the other” [CH2.4]

Mind

The book spells it: Mind, MIND, mind, Minde

  1. The part of man from God, by which he is united with God: steersman of the soul; the soul turned into it draws near God, sees all things and needs no outward teaching; the man who lives in it alone is as an angel; held as the Image of Divinity, it brings blessedness. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “He hath a Spirit or Mind from God; for what else is the Spirit of Man which God breathed into him but God himselfe dwelling in us?” [MICRO.24]
    • “The mind raised up into the most High God and united with him converseth with God, and doth the same works” [MICRO.25]
    • “Stern-man of the Soul or Rationall Spirit, & like the eternall God concludeth all Beings, Times and Places” [N117]
    • “Man whose mind by Christ is united to God, possesseth the true wisdome of all things” [MICRO.9]
    • “If the Soule run back within it selfe and be converted into the Mind it draweth near to God, seeth all things, and hath no need of outward teaching” [CH5.23]
    • “the Man that liveth in nothing but the Mind, is as an Angel” [CH6.68]
    • “embracing its Image of Divinity or MIND united to GOD by Christ, we enjoy actuall Blessednesse” [CH6.68]
  2. One MIND above every mind, in which all minds shall at last be one thing. Several readings: (1) God, since the same passage hastens to that which is one and perfect and goes on to call it the intimate vision of God; (2) only a oneness of all minds, the passage naming it no further. (veiled · several · voice: CROLL)
    • “all minds, by an unanimous friendship, after an unutterable manner, shall be altogether but one thing, in one MIND which is above every MIND” [CH6.68]
    • “they hasten from the imperfect to that which is one and perfect” [CH6.68]
    • “It is the intimate vision of GOD, and the Intuitive knowledge of GOD” [CH6.68]
  3. The earthly mind, named with Limus Terrae, after which the friends of God do not live. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “not after the Limus Terrae the Eearthy Mind” [CH6.68]

Mine

The book spells it: Mine

  1. The place the matter of the Elixer is found; Morienes says the king is in it, so a man is meant. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Morienes · wording-dependent)
    • “and thou are in its Mine” [CH3.36]
  2. The mine out of which the Naturall Mercuriality of the four Elements, of little worth, is got by art; the sentence sets that Mercuriality in man. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: Riplaeus à Portis · wording-dependent)
    • “Remember that Man is the most noble Creature of all, in whom there is the Naturall Mercuriality of the four Elements” [CH3.37]
    • “which is of little worth, and may be got out of its Mine by art” [CH3.37]

Minister

The book spells it: Minister, minister, ministers

  1. The physician as servant of Nature and of God, who separates and administers the power he received from God, and is not Nature’s master. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “The Duty of Natures Minister, the Physitian.” [CH5.0]
    • “A true Phisitian should be the minister, not a master to Nature” [CH5.7]
    • “Nature hath commanded the Physitians to be ministers, and to seperate, not masters to compose” [CH3.6]
    • “But to the Physitian who is the obedient Minister of God and Nature” [CH2.23]
    • “he hath duely administred the power which he received from God” [CH2.23]
    • “What manner of Phisitian that Phisick doth require for its Minister.” [OPEN.28]
  2. Death called God’s servant, waiting on our intestine war. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “Death Gods minister expecteth our intestine war.” [N307]

Mummy

The book spells it: Mummy

  1. the true Medicine, called natural Mummy and kernel of Nature, held in the vital Sulphur (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “this Medicine, as naturall Mummy and kernell of Nature, is contained in the vitall Sulphur” [CH1.3]
  2. man’s innate Mummy: named with the inward Nature or Balsom and with the vitall spirit that has its seat in the midst of man’s heart; by it Nature heals its own infirmities; its destruction is ultimate death; in the Plague it is infected, with the Sulphur of man (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “this very selfe same Nature, which is our Life and Balsom, or balsomicall Mummy, that preserveth the body from all corruption” [CH4.3]
    • “it selfe healeth its own infirmities by the innate Mummy” [CH4.6]
    • “the innate Mummy, and hath its seat in the midst of Mans heart” [CH6.60]
    • “the Balsamick Mummy, the Vitall Body, the liquor of Life” [CH6.62]
    • “ultimate death the most Terrible of all Terrible things (which is the destruction of the Mummy)” [CH6.71]
    • “it infecteth the Mummy and Sulphur of the microcosm, Man” [CH5.75]
  3. the Word of God as a supercaelestiall Mummy, set above man’s own Mummy (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “it is the supercaelestiall Mummy, the supernaturall Balsome comforting poor Mortalls more then Mans own Mummy or naturall Balsom” [CH2.19]
  4. Mummy as a thing that stops bleeding in man, because of the like Anotomy (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “because of the like Anotomy it is that Mummy will stop the bleeding in Man” [CH1.38]
  5. named with Salt as one thing that can be spent; the note joins its spending to nothing but wormes (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL · note-only · wording-dependent)
    • “When the Salt or Mummy is spent things br{G022: 2 letters illegible}ed nothing but wormes.” [N053]

Mystery

The book spells it: Mystery, mystery, mysteries, Mysteries, Misteries

  1. The healing part of a thing, which chymistry separates out; at CH3.14 named the Quint-Essence. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “it teacheth to segregate every mystery into into its own reservacle” [CH3.7]
    • “the Soul the Inhabitant thereof, the Super-elementall mystery the Quint-Essence” [CH3.14]
  2. The hidden things of God and of Nature, which true philosophers come to understand and imitate and which impostors are unworthy of. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “really to imitate the greatest mysteries and secrets of Nature” [CH5.10]
    • “his marvellous works, wonders and mysteries which he hath revealed” [CH3.13]
    • “as ignorant and unworthy of the mysteries of God, and the secrets of Nature” [OPEN.11]
    • “no perfect perception of Mysteries can be attained to” [CH6.22]
    • “though divine Misteries are far greater than to be set forth by the splendor of mans words” [CH6.76]
  3. The soul’s turning back within itself into the Mind, by which it draws near to God; closed to the academic spirit, open to humility, and not to be spoken of openly in this place. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “If the Soule run back within it selfe and be converted into the Mind it draweth near to God” [CH5.23]
    • “The Academicall spirit cannot not understand this Mystery” [CH5.23]
    • “nor may I in this unfit place openly speak more of these Mysteries” [CH5.24]
  4. The Phylosophers’ work, whose matter is had everywhere because it is in every elementated thing. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: QUOTED: the Phylosophers)
    • “the Phylosophers say that the matter of their Mystery may be had every where, because it consisteth in every Elementated thing” [CH3.34]

Natural death

The book spells it: Naturall death, Naturall Death, naturall death

  1. Death by the wasting of natural moisture and heat, the full dissipation of the vital spirit after old age, which diseases can cause; spoken of philosophically, apart from the fatal or ultimate death God fixed. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “I speak here Phylosophically of the Naturall death, which is onely the wasting of the Naturall Moysture and Heat, as may be seen in a burning lamp” [CH6.70]
    • “not Theologically of the Fatall death and utmost Terme which God hath prefixt to every one” [CH6.70]
    • “at last followeth a full extinction, consumption and dissipation of that spirit, which is the Naturall Death” [CH6.56]
    • “chase away from us all diseases which cause the naturall death, untill ultimate death the most Terrible of all Terrible things” [CH6.71]
  2. The term of life God appointed to man for disobedience, up to which the Medicine keeps a man sound; here natural death is equated with that term. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “even to his Naturall death, that is to that Terme of Life which the Omnipotent God hath appointed to Man for disobedience” [CH6.54]

Natural Faith

The book spells it: NATURALL FAITH, Naturall Faith, faith of Nature

  1. An inborn faith or wisdom God the Father gave equally to all men at the Creation, distinct from the faith of grace by Christ; the text says it is our Imagination, that it can cause and cure diseases, and that its efficacy showed in the woman with the bloody issue and in the Centurion. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Secondly, by NATURALL FAITH, or in-born Wisdome, which God the Father equally communicated to all men in the Creation” [MICRO.39]
    • “Naturall Faith (I speak not of the Faith of Grace by Christ) inborn of God the Father in the first Creation, which is our IMAGINATION, is so powerfull that it can both cause and cure diseases” [CH5.75]
    • “Againe, the aforesaid Naturall Faith its efficacy manifested it selfe in the woman that had the bloody issue, and in the Centurion.” [CH5.79]
    • “the efficacy of the faith of Nature, which by God can do all things Naturally” [N244]

Natural heat

The book spells it: Naturall Heat

  1. The heat in man that digests nourishment; the text makes it the heat of the Sun and Moon in us and one with the celestial spirit when that spirit is unhindered in the body. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “our Naturall Heat, or the microcosmicall heat of the Sun and Moon that is in us, whereby the Nutriment is digested” [CH5.44]
    • “This Celestiall Spirit which is one and the same with our Naturall spirit, when its breathing in our body is not lesned or hindred by outward things, is that Naturall Heat of ours” [CH6.56]
    • “The Heat of the Sun and Moon is that Naturall Heat whereby every thing is digested for the sustentation and multiplication” [N288]
    • “strengthen, inflame and augment the weak Naturall Heat or Fire” [CH6.60]

Natural life

The book spells it: Naturall Life, Naturall life

  1. The life of natural things, which is the acting of the Elements and comes from God; the Medicine brings a beam of it to the heart. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “for the Naturall life is nothing else but the actings of the Elements.” [N281]
    • “The Naturall life is from God the fountaine of the Universall life.” [CH2.12]
    • “conveying to the Heart a beam of the Naturall Life, and renewing the organs of Life” [CH6.38]

Natural term

The book spells it: Naturall Terme, Naturall Term

  1. The end of life a man reaches when not cut short by disease, accident or corruption from the womb; few reach it. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “But as few reach the Naturall Terme, so also few have known the reason of prolonging the life” [CH6.72]
    • “may we be hindred and kept from attaining to the Naturall Term of life” [CH6.74]
  2. Simply the end of a man’s life, here reached by a violent death by poison. Interpreted: the phrase is used of an untimely death, against the first sense. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL · wording-dependent)
    • “he came to the Naturall Terme of his Life by an untimely and violent death of a draught of Poyson” [CH6.62]

Nature

The book spells it: Nature, nature, NATURE

  1. The medium that joins the lowest things to the highest by harmonious consent, called animal, vegetable or mineral according to the diversity of the subject or receptacle. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Nature, I say, is that medium which by an Harmonicall consent joyneth the lowest things to the highest” [CH3.32]
    • “sometimes is called Animall, sometimes Vegetable, sometimes Minerall, according to the diversity of the subject or receptacle” [CH3.32]
  2. The order and series of God’s works, obeying God and borrowing all its virtue from him. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “which is the order and series of Gods works, and obey the power, word and command of God, and borroweth all its vertue and efficacy from him” [CH3.40]
  3. The one Nature that came forth as the essence and matter of all things and the Specifick of every creature. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “that one onely Nature the Essence and Matter of all things” [CH3.30]
    • “which Nature is the Specifick of every Creature” [CH3.30]
  4. The invisible Fire or fiery vigour by which all things are multiplied. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “is the invisible Fire or fiery vigour by which all things are multiplyed” [N178]
  5. The healing power within the body: the genuine physician of all diseases, the Internall Mummy or inward Balsom, a virtue and not a quality; the physician is her servant and minister. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Thus Nature is not onely our companion, but our friend and ready helper, it alone being the genuine Physitian of all diseases” [CH4.6]
    • “Nature her selfe (who is the Internall Mummy or inward Balsom)” [CH4.6]
    • “because Nature is not a quality but a vertue” [CH4.6]
    • “by her servant the Physitian” [CH4.6]
    • “A true Phisitian should be the minister, not a master to Nature” [CH5.7]
  6. The present order of the world, which gives nothing pure but mixes all things with impurities, to put men in mind to learn chymistry. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Nature therefore, as it is now, gives us nothing that is pure in the world, but hath mixed all things with many impurities” [CH3.12]
    • “it might often put us in mind that we should begin to learn the knowledge of Chymistry from our cradles” [CH3.12]
  7. Nature as a book and a sanctuary whose secrets the true philosophers and physicians read and enter, whose Light teaches like a sound Doctour; Art and Nature consent like mother and daughter, Art imitating her and supplying her defects. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Light of Nature, which like a sound Doctour teacheth and instructeth men” [CH1.1]
    • “the BOOK of NATURE (in which every region is a leaf)” [CH6.76]
    • “they go into the very inner roomes and Sanctuary of Nature” [CH5.7]
    • “because Art and Nature, like mother and daughter, consent to each other” [CH2.5]
    • “Art imitateth Nature and supplyeth its defects” [N136]
  8. Heraclitus’s name for Nature: the daughter of War. (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Heraclitus)
    • “Heraclitus called Nature the daughter of War” [CH3.5]
  9. The nature of each thing, which preserves itself and partakes by affinity of the nature of another. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “every Nature is Naturally the best preserver of it selfe” [CH4.6]
    • “if the nature of one thing did not by affinity partake of the nature of another” [CH3.30]

New Birth

The book spells it: New Birth

  1. A rebirth to be sought first, after which all other natural things are added without much labour; called the field of heavenly physick that heals with a word. Interpreted (my inference), narrowly: the regeneration a margin note names, with faith, as that by which all sciences are perfectly attained. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “The New Birth is the Field of Caelestiall Physick which healeth with a word without Externall means” [CH2.22]
    • “Wherefore the New Birth is first to be sought for, and then all other Naturall things will be added without much labour.” [CH5.13]
    • “All Sciences are perfectly attained unto by the Fundamentall of Faith and new Regeneration or Celestiall Transplantation” [N203]

Nothing

The book spells it: Nothing, Nothingnesse

  1. Man’s own Nothing: what man comes to by turning from God to himself, joined with his own evil; a margin note says whatever is not God is Nothing. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “learned by experience what his own proper Evill and Nothing was to his voluntary damage and perdition” [MICRO.10]
    • “we should slide into our selves or the Creatures as claiming to our selves the property both of them and of our selves” [CH5.22]
    • “fall back into that vaine, wicked or our own Nothing” [CH5.22]
    • “Whatsoever is not God, is Nothing, and ought to be esteemed as nothing.” [N299]
  2. The most beautiful Nothing: the shadow and region of darkness and death, to be cast behind on returning to the Being of Beings. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “till we have cast behind us the most beautifull Nothing (that is, the shadow and region of Darknesse and Death)” [CH6.68]
  3. A Sea of Nothingness into which the friends of God cast themselves from divine love, with the fountain of the Abyss. Interpreted (this reading’s own inference): a nothingness sought, not fled. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “cast themselves headlong into the fountaine of the Abysse and into the Sea of Nihilitude or Nothingnesse” [CH6.68]

Olympic spirit

The book spells it: Olimpick spirit, Olimpick Spirit, Olympick Spirit

  1. The inner Heaven of Man, the invisible or Sydereall Man, which holds all the Astra’s and is the same with the Firmament of the Astra’s; the author says that had Aristotle and Galen taken account of it, such errors had not crept into Philosophy and Physick. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the internall Heaven of Man, which is the Olimpick spirit, doth particularly comprehend all the Astra’s” [CH1.14]
    • “The invisible man or Olimpick Spirit is borne in us after this manner.” [MICRO.31]
    • “If Aristotle had taken notice of this Olympick Spirit, and Galen made more account of it, there had not such errors crept into Philosophy and Physick” [MICRO.31]
    • “the Sydereall, Internall, Olimpick. Incorporeall, or (Gabalis Homo) coagulated or curdled Man is the same with the Firmament of the Astra’s” [MICRO.32]

Opinion

The book spells it: Opinion, opinions

  1. Belief held without tested truth, set against the truth found by experiment; named with Scorne as one of the “lets” (OPEN.17) of learning, cast away by those who come to the truth, and said in the margin to rule the world. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “having first cast away Opinion and Scorne the lets of learning” [OPEN.17]
    • “The world is ruled by Opinion.” [N029]
    • “not fallacious opinions” [OPEN.3]
    • “but the truth it self so long desired, by many experiments most certainly found out and known” [OPEN.3]

Paracelsus

The book spells it: Paracelsus, Paracels.

  1. An author the text cites and follows as an authority in Physick and philosophy, whose due place in practice and preparations it claims; the margin says only the physician who follows his mind can open the Book of Nature, and gives a word of his a sense of his own (“the Salt of Paracelsus”, N194). (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “as you may find in the Sage and deep Phylosophy of Paracelsus” [CH1.40]
    • “give place to Paracelsus according to his desert in practice and preparations” [OPEN.21]
    • “that Physitian that walketh according to the mind of Paracelsus is onely able to open the Book of Nature” [N316]
    • “When Paracelsus saith that like preserve their like, and are destroyed by their contraryes” [CH4.6]
    • “All Terresterity is the Salt of Paracelsus.” [N194]

Perfect metals

The book spells it: perfect Mettalls, perfect mettalls

  1. Metals in which, above all, God has written in sublunary things, and whose Spirits free man’s body from corruption as Vegetables cannot; the clause “which are not subject to corruptions” (CH3.22) follows them. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “upon which God hath written with his own sacred finger in sublunary things, but especially in perfect Mettalls” [CH1.1]
    • “Nor can it be that Vegetables which are obnoxious to corruption should so free Mans body from corruption, as the Spirits of perfect mettalls doe, which are not subject to corruptions” [CH3.22]

Philosophists

The book spells it: Phylosophists

  1. Would-be philosophers who are impatient of the long work, greedy and boastful like mountebanks, and without the liberal arts; set beside impostors. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “but with much tediousnesse to Phylosophists who are impatient of delay” [CH3.41]
    • “as those covètous Mountebanks and greedy Phylosophists use to doe” [CH6.4]
    • “of which those Phylosophists and Imposters are altogether destitute, having not tasted so much as a drop of the Springs of Nature” [CH6.22]

Philosophy

The book spells it: Phylosophy, Philosophy

  1. The knowledge of earth and water, as Astronomy is of the firmament and air (or fire and air); with Astronomy it makes a perfect philosopher of the great and the little world. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Phylosophy and Astronomy make up an internall and perfect Phylosopher” [CH1.4]
    • “Phylosophy teacheth the vertues and qualityes of the Earth and Water, as Astronomy doth of the fire and Ayre” [CH5.9]
  2. A practical medicine that helps the physician to his remedies; called the mother of physicians. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “For Phylosophy is a practicall Physicke helping the Physitian to any medicine in a readynesse” [CH5.12]
    • “Phylosophy is the mother of Physitians” [N202]
  3. True philosophy: the mind’s right knowledge of itself, grounded on Scripture and Christ and returning into God; at its end, knowing God and passing into him so as to be made, as it were, a God; it goes together with godliness and theology. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the most excellent Phylosophy is that which enlightens the mind to the right knowledge of it selfe” [MICRO.1]
    • “All true Phylosophy should be grounded on the Scriptures and so return into God” [MICRO.8]
    • “True Phylosophy must be grounded on Christ the corner stone” [MICRO.8]
    • “this is true Theologicall Phylosophy” [CH5.13]
    • “Without Phylosophy it is impossible to be absolutely godly” [CH5.19]
    • “To know GOD himselfe the maker of all things, and passe into him with a full image of his likenesse” [CH6.66]
    • “this at last will prove the True and sollid Phylosophy” [CH6.66]
  4. The philosophy of the Heathen: called polluted and corrupt, the spring of error, not to rule Christians. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “because their Phylosophy and other abilities were polluted and corrupt” [MICRO.8]
    • “Christians should not be ruled by Heathen Phylosophy.” [N089]
    • “much lesse our Students of Heathenish Phylosophy from whence all errour springeth” [CH5.12]

Physic

The book spells it: Physick, Phisick

  1. The art, study and profession of healing. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “in the practice of Physick” [OPEN.3]
    • “the Titles and Honours of Philosophy and Phisick” [OPEN.14]
    • “thrust themselves into the profession of Physick with unwashen hands” [CH5.59]
  2. Healing as God’s gift: the created and incarnate Mercy of God, set in things beside his Justice, which is the disease and venom. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Physick is nothing else but the created and incarnate Mercy of our Heavenly Father” [CH1.2]
    • “the Mercy of God is the Physick in Nature and in all things” [N163]
    • “Physick is a favour given of God” [N038]
  3. The healing power hidden in things, wrapped in coverings and to be separated from them; a Spirit, not a body, discerned only by the wise man. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the true medicine (or Physick) is wrapt up in rindes, barks, matrixes, receptacles, husks, garment and cottages” [CH2.1]
    • “it is of necessity that the same must be seperated from the impure Elements by the artificiall Anotomy of Chymists” [CH2.1]
    • “Physick is a Spirit (not a body) which the Magician or wise man only can discerne” [CH4.6]
  4. The work of curing itself: refreshing the strength and Balsom and removing the impurities that cause diseases. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “For Physick is nothing else but an opposition of those things that are desired, to wit, a refreshing of the strength and Balsom, and a removing of superfluityes or impurities that cause diseases” [CH4.2]

Poison

The book spells it: Poyson, poyson, Poysoned

  1. A hurtful thing that kills, as the draught given to Paracelsus by his adversaries. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “being Poysoned in his body by his malicious and inhuman adversaries” [CH6.62]
    • “by an untimely and violent death of a draught of Poyson” [CH6.62]
  2. The hurtful part set in every thing of the lower world close beside its physick or healing part, to be separated from it; at CH5.37 called a Tartarous Mucilaginous Impurity. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “nothing in this lower world (which is subject to generation and corruption) is so noble but hath Poyson in it selfe and in respect of another very near the Essence or Physick” [CH3.1]
    • “hath Poyson or a Tartarous Mucilaginous Impurity hard by or close unto the medicine or Physick thereof” [CH5.37]
    • “Poyson from healing Medicines and Balsoms” [CH3.14]
  3. The poison God set in the same place as the physick he gave, as a guard over it. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Roger Bacon)
    • “in the same place or thing where of his MERCY he appoynted plenty of Physick, even there also by the power of his incomprehensible Justice he substituted Poyson to guard it” [CH3.3]
  4. A venomous power that can overcome another poison: Nature destroys one poison by another, as opium’s greater degree of poison overcomes a pestilence’s; each poisonous creature holds an antidote to its own poison. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “there is no poysonous Creature but hath in it an Antidote against its own poyson” [CH3.14]
    • “by the Specifick venemous vertue of the Opium which hath a greater degree of poyson” [CH4.6]
    • “Thus Nature destroyeth one poyson by another” [CH4.6]
  5. A name Paracelsus gives, with Tartar, to the excrements of meat and drink that for the most part breed all diseases. (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Paracelsus)
    • “which Paracelsus comprehendeth under the name of Poyson and Tartar” [CH5.43]
    • “these Excrements of meat and drink, which for the most part breed all our diseases” [CH5.43]

Quintessence

The book spells it: Quintessence, Quint-Essence

  1. What God took out of all created things to make man; and man is called the Quintessence of the greater world, who may rule Heaven, not only imitate it. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “took the Quintessence out of all things created and thereof fashioned and composed Man as the Prince and End of all these” [MICRO.12]
    • “whereas Man is the Quintessence of the greater world, it follows that Man may not onely imitate Heaven, but rule it also” [MICRO.36]
  2. In medicine: the soul that dwells in a medicine, its super-elemental mystery and the true inner balsam of our bodies, separated by art and fire from its body, husks and dregs. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “from the Soul the Inhabitant thereof, the Super-elementall mystery the Quint-Essence, which is the true Internall, sutable, freindly and correspondent Balsom of our bodyes” [CH3.14]
    • “so doth Fire the good from the bad, the Quint-Essence from the body” [N171]
  3. The Quint-Essence of Gold: a medicine that renews the whole body. (veiled · Stated · voice: UNCLEAR)
    • “onely by the Quint-Essence of Gold which reneweth the whole body” [CH5.36]

Receipts

The book spells it: Receipts

  1. Prescriptions, of which the author will not fill a huge volume with copies, and under whose weight physicians are almost pressed to death; the margin calls the greatest of them for the most part deceits. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “to fill up a huge Volume with the copies of large Receipts” [OPEN.3]
    • “prest to death under so heavy a weight of their Receipts” [OPEN.3]
    • “The greatest Receipts the greatest Deceits for the most part.” [N005]

Receptacle

The book spells it: Receptacle, receptacle

  1. The place or subject in which the one seed or the one Nature is, by whose difference it differs and is called animal, vegetable or mineral. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “differ onely in Quality of Place and Receptacle” [CH3.28]
    • “according to the disposition and variety of the Place and Receptacle” [CH3.30]
    • “sometimes is called Animall, sometimes Vegetable, sometimes Minerall, according to the diversity of the subject or receptacle” [CH3.32]

Regeneration

The book spells it: Regeneration

  1. In the Chymist’s work: the second life a herb or medicine reaches after its first life dies, through putrefaction, in which the three first show their hidden virtues; without it no hid secret of physic is reached. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “’Tis necessary that the first life of hearbs and medicines should die that the second life by the Chymists help may be attained through Putrefaction and Regeneration” [CH1.37]
    • “for without Regeneration no hid Secret of Physick can be attained to” [CH1.37]
  2. The state of the chief medicine, called a whole new regenerate world, by virtue of which it renews man, the little old world. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “this whole new regenerate world is able by vertue of its Regeneration to renew the little old corruptible world Man” [CH6.38]
    • “the Fift Essence of the whole frame of the world, and the whole world Regenerated” [CH6.51]
  3. That by which Christians have their seed from God, which the Heathen have not; it overcomes a bad birth, is glossed as a celestial transplantation, and gives true philosophy and all sciences; Hermes and others knew it before the Word was incarnate and concealed it. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Christians onely in whom the Truth is planted, who have their seed from God, by the means of Regeneration which the Heathen have not” [MICRO.8]
    • “No Art can be perfected without Regeneration” [MICRO.8]
    • “This Regeneration that holy man Hermes and others of clean hearts and godly lives before the Word was incarnate being enlightned by the holy Spirit” [MICRO.9]
    • “though they concealed it among other Secrets, they knew it better then many of us who call our selves Christians” [MICRO.9]
    • “Regeneration overcometh a bad birth” [N102]
    • “All Sciences are perfectly attained unto by the Fundamentall of Faith and new Regeneration or Celestiall Transplantation” [N203]
    • “A man cannot have better Phylosophy then f{G162: 1 letter illegible}om God in the Regeneration” [N204]
    • “a desire of future Blessednesse through the Regeneration” [CH5.18]
  4. The Regeneration according to soul, spirit and body that will sparkle out through the body at the end of this world, with the New Jerusalem named in a bracket after it; the time of Regeneration is said to draw nigh. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “at the end of this world which is to be Renewed by the Ternary of Man” [CH5.21]
    • “the Regeneration according to Soul, Spirit, and Body (the New Jerusalem, wherein the Unitrine Essence of God and the Holy and most Holy Trinity inseperably dwell) will sparkle and glitter out” [CH5.21]
    • “the time of Revocation and Regeneration drawing nigh, every imperfect thing will of necessity come to nought” [CH3.46]

Resolution

The book spells it: Resolution, resolution

  1. The dissolving of a body, artificial or Spagiricall, which brings to light the properties that lay in its composition; by a Naturall and due Resolution the power of gold would be brought into act. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “By this artificall resolution of bodyes the propertyes which before layd in the compositions of them are now brought to light.” [CH3.8]
    • “as in a Spagiricall resolution is plainly to be seen” [CH1.19]
    • “had its power reduced into act by a Naturall and due Resolution, that it might exercise its vertues” [CH2.5]
  2. A dissolving bearing on the body’s matter: one of the four ways Salt destroys health; the Salt of the urine forced into resolution by separation; and the turning of what strengthens natural heat into a simple spirit, done by resolution and conjunction with the Nutriment. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Salt destroyeth health 4 wayes, by Resolution, Calcination” [CH4.8]
    • “forced into resolution by the violence of separation” [CH5.34]
    • “which is done by resolution and conjuction with the Nutriment” [CH5.44]
  3. The resolution of the Iliad: a cause of death the physician’s industry may hinder, unlike death from the Ens or Being. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Paracelsus)
    • “Therefore Paracelsus saith that the death which is from the resolution of the Iliad may be hindred by the industry of the Physitian, but that which is from the Ens or Being cannot” [CH6.71]

Sabbath

The book spells it: Sabbath

  1. An inward rest from temporal and earthly things in which man turns to God and lives with him. Interpreted from its pairing with ‘oblivion of Temporall things’, from ‘Sabbath of their Heart’, and from ‘Rest and Blessednesse’. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL · wording-dependent)
    • “were supprest by the Sabbath and oblivion of Temporall things” [CH5.20]
    • “be turned to God in the Sabbath of their Heart by a separation of the Minde from Terrene obsticles in themselves” [CH6.68]
    • “that in the Sabbath they may live with God in Rest and Blessednesse” [CH6.68]

Salt

The book spells it: Salt

  1. One of the three Principles of every natural body, beside Mercury and Sulphur: it gives solidity, colour and taste and remains in the ashes when green wood burns; a bitter hot Salt is found in Opium by separating its substances, and the superfluity of Salt in the body is separated by the urine. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “Into Mercury or liquor, Sulphur or Oyle, and Salt” [CH1.19]
    • “The Salt by coagulation gives Solidity, Colour and Tast to all bodies” [CH1.19]
    • “The third, viz. the Salt is unctuous, very little, thin and lasting, and remains in the ashes.” [CH1.19]
    • “a bitter hot Salt penetrating by a subtile resolution and causing sweat” [CH3.19]
    • “the superfluity of Salt is separated by the Urine” [CH5.33]
  2. Salt in a wider use of another writer, as the author reads his drift: whatever will melt, may be resolved into a watery moisture, and is then dried and hardened by fire; the margin beside it says all Terresterity is the Salt of Paracelsus. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “sometime he calls that Salt whatever it be that will melt, and may be resolved into a watry moysture, and then dryed and hardened by Fire” [CH4.9]
    • “All Terresterity is the Salt of Paracelsus.” [N194]
  3. Salt named beside Mummy (‘the Salt or Mummy’), in a margin note on what follows when the Salt or Mummy is spent. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL · note-only)
    • “When the Salt or Mummy is spent” [N053]

Secret

The book spells it: Secret, Secrets, secrets

  1. Hidden things which the ancient Philosophers forbade to be profaned and made known to unworthy men. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: ancient Philosophers)
    • “strictly forbidden to prophane the Secrets” [OPEN.2]
  2. Hidden things kept from the common sort of those who profess philosophy; the author names his own publication as bringing such secrets to light. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “These most secret of secrets have ever been hid from the common sort of them that professe Phylosophy” [CH6.71]
    • “pryed into these excellent secrets” [OPEN.10]
    • “should so soon be brought to light, and made common to every one” [OPEN.10]
  3. A remedy: the text sets secrets beside medicines and remedies, and says secrets work without matter and body. My inference: a secret is a hidden medicine or its working power. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “these chief secrets of the more hidden Medicines here offered” [OPEN.6]
    • “by administring such Remedies and secrets as are derived from the fountaine of Gods gifts” [CH6.71]
    • “Wherefore we want nothing but the knowledge of Secrets, and their use.” [CH6.71]
    • “Such is the Nature of all Secrets that they worke without the matter and body because the diseases also are not bodyes” [CH4.6]
    • “the power and operation of the Secret” [CH3.14]
    • “may be the greatest Secret for the removing all diseases” [CH6.62]
    • “those things which they bragged of as great secrets” [OPEN.3]
  4. The one chief universal Physick, called the most secret Secret, by which all things may be done. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “that chief and universal Phylosophical Phisick” [CH6.1]
    • “this most secret Secret of secret Secrets, by which even wonders, yea all things may be done” [CH6.1]
  5. The greatest Terrene Treasure entrusted to the adept to keep, which evil men would abuse to the ruin of the whole world. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “this greatest and richest Terrene Treasure” [CH6.27]
    • “which Secret would by evill men, with the ruine of the whole world, be abused” [CH6.27]
  6. Hidden things that lie in God and in Nature, of which God is the first inventor; the man united to God knows them all. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “those Secrets which are both in God and Nature” [OPEN.17]
    • “who being the first inventer of all Secrets” [CH1.1]
    • “the most absolute knowledge of all Secrets” [MICRO.9]
    • “a keeper of Gods Secrets” [CH6.27]

Secret forger

The book spells it: secret Forger, secret forger

  1. The inner Astrum or syderiall spirit in each thing, from which its formation, figure, colour and smell proceed from within, not from without; a margin note likens the formation of things in the Astra’s to iron in the imagination of the Smith. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “a syderiall spirit, which is called Heaven or the Astrum, the secret Forger, from which every Formation, Figure and Colour of things proceedeth” [CH1.15]
    • “neither smell nor colour, nor forme, but all things proceed from the inner Astrum or secret forger, and not from without” [CH1.16]
    • “as iron in the imagination of the Smith” [N047]

Secretaries of nature

The book spells it: Secretaries of Nature

  1. Those who hold nature’s secrets: the private ones count nothing more glorious than to rejoice in quiet silence, the public ones are exposed to dangers. Interpreted from the contrast of ‘private’ and ‘publick’ in the two places. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “these private not publick Secretaries of Nature” [CH6.46]
    • “accounting nothing more glorious than that they might peaceably rejoyce prudently and in Quiet Silence” [CH6.46]
    • “knowing to what dangers the searchers out of difficult Arts and the publick Secretaries of Nature are obnoxious” [CH6.50]

Secrets of Nature

The book spells it: secrets of Nature, Secrets of Nature

  1. The hidden things of Nature, which true philosophers and skilled physicians search out, understand and imitate; finding them out is called the chief temporal thing and the end of Physick, and the cheats are called unworthy of them. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “men skill’d in the secrets of Nature” [OPEN.14]
    • “as ignorant and unworthy of the mysteries of God, and the secrets of Nature” [OPEN.11]
    • “they doe at last attaine not onely to understand, but also really to imitate the greatest mysteries and secrets of Nature” [CH5.10]
    • “in temporalls the chiefest thing is to find out the secrets of Nature” [N032]
    • “the end of Phisick is not to scrape up money, but to discover the secrets of Nature” [N034]
    • “not onely to come all the desired Secrets of Nature” [CH6.35]

Seed

The book spells it: seed, seeds, Seeds, Seed

  1. The seed of a plant, from which it grows and in which another of its kind lies hid. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “As a Tree groweth from the seed, the seed is the beginning of the Tree, and the seed also is the end of the Tree” [MICRO.17]
  2. The Astrum, the invisible body in a thing, which brings forth a visible body; the Earth holds the seeds of all things, and each of its fruits is again the Astrum and Seed. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the seeds of any graine or Wheat is the Astrum, viz. the invisible body, which being cast into the Earth it produceth a visible body” [MICRO.32]
    • “every one of these fruits is againe the Astrum and Seed” [CH1.29]
    • “Though the Earth seem to be dead, yet hath it in it selfe the seeds and seminall vertues of all things” [CH1.29]
    • “the fruits of those Astra’s or Caelestiall, Ayry, Earthy, Watry seeds doe indeavour and bend to one generall Good” [CH1.17]
  3. In the passage given out of Severinus: the Seeds that, with the Astra’s, lay hid in the Elements from the beginning of the Creation, which bring forth what was treasured up in them, open the use of the Elements, and imitate the oeconomy of the world. (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Severinus)
    • “to the Seeds that they may produce out of themselves the things that were secretly treasured up in them” [CH1.22]
    • “The Seeds and Astra’s, those bonds of things, lay hid in the invisible Treasures of the Elements from the beginning of the Creation” [CH1.23]
    • “The knowledge of the Elements cannot be attained unto without these Seeds, because they declare or open the use and services of the Elements” [CH1.23]
    • “all the least and smallest Seeds strive to imitate the oeconomy of the world, and hold forth a dark resemblance of the Elements and Principles” [CH1.25]
  4. The first, pure seeds of things, in which the roots of sicknesses are implanted and mixed; the nourishments of natural things are the fruits of those seeds, which spring up in the four Elements as wombs. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “implanted and mixt in the pure and first seeds of things” [CH3.11]
    • “the nourishments of Naturall things are the fruits of those seeds which spring up in the foure wombs or Elements” [CH3.11]
  5. The origin from which a whole world comes: the first matter is the seed of all created things, the great world the seed of Man, and the Word FIAT the seed of the whole world. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “so the great world is the seed of Adam or Man” [MICRO.17]
    • “So the First Matter (which Paracelsus calleth the Limbus, whose Earth was the WORD of the Lord) was the seed of all things that were to be created” [MICRO.17]
    • “which is the generall seed of all things or subject of the first matter” [CH3.29]
    • “by this Word, FIAT, the seed of the whole world, were Heaven and Earth created” [CH2.20]
  6. Man as the last and perfect seed of the world, able to beget another man; the text itself says Man is not a seed as other seed is. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “and Man was the last of all as the perfect seed, which againe is able out of himselfe to beget another Man like himself” [MICRO.17]
    • “And though Man be not a seed as other seed is, yet hath he power to cast seed out of himselfe” [MICRO.17]
  7. The seeds of all things, which lie hid in Man; a margin note, in the author’s own words, calls Man that Earth or field which has all seeds in itself. (veiled · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Interpreters of Secret Nature)
    • “yea and that the seeds of all things lye hid in him” [CH2.4]
    • “that Earth or field which hath all seeds in it self” [N106]
  8. Interpreted: the truth planted from God, by Regeneration, in which true Philosophy grows; among the Gentiles that seed was choked for want of the Sun. The basis: the same paragraph says the Truth is planted in Christians who have their seed from God, by Regeneration. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “that seed which among the Gentiles was choaked for want of the Sun, like that among the thorns” [MICRO.8]
    • “Christians onely in whom the Truth is planted, who have their seed from God, by the means of Regeneration which the Heathen have not” [MICRO.8]

Separation

The book spells it: seperation

  1. The parting of purity from impurity, or truth from falsehood, given as the meaning of a Spagiricall resolution. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “A seperation of purity from impurity, or truth from falshood” [N052]
    • “as in a Spagiricall resolution is plainly to be seen” [CH1.19]
  2. The parting of strong from weak and pure from impure that can be made in certain bodies. (plain · Stated · voice: QUOTED: Severinus)
    • “in such bodies there may be a seperation made of the strong from the weak, of the pure from the impure” [CH1.25]
  3. The act by which the spiritual Elements, in the separation of all things, set the visible bodies apart from themselves, as the soul parts its body from itself and yet dwells in it. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “so also these spirituall Elements in the seperation of all things have severed the visible bodies from themselves by seperation” [CH1.27]
  4. A work of each bodily member, named beside its digestion, emunctory and excrement (margin note only). (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “Every member hath its own proper digestion, seperation, emunctory, excrement in it selfe” [N217]

Sidereal body

The book spells it: Sydereall body, sydereall body

  1. Man’s second, invisible body, from the Astra’s of the Firmament and living by it, the house of the spirit’s life and capable of philosophy, as against the visible body from the Earth. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the Physicall body of Man, which is nourished from the Earth, and Sydereall body which liveth by the Firmament” [CH1.37]
    • “but the Invisible, Insensible, Sydereall body (which is the house or cottage of the Spirits life) is from the Astra’s of the Firmament” [MICRO.31]
    • “Thus Adam had two bodies, that is, a visible Elementall, and an invisible Sydereall body” [MICRO.31]
    • “but the invisible Sydereall body that is capable of attaining Phylosophy is from the Astra’s of the Firmament” [MICRO.31]
    • “Now whereas Man had his Sydereall body from the Astra’s of the Firmament” [MICRO.35]
  2. Something every thing, sensible or insensible, has in it, named beside the sydereall spirit (margin note only). (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “That which is sensible and that which is insensible hath a sydereall spirit or a sydereall body in it” [N120]

Sidereal spirit

The book spells it: Sydereall Spirit, Sydereall spirit, sydereall spirit, Syderiall spirit, syderiall spirit, syderiall Spirit, Syderiall Spirit

  1. The invisible part of a star, the Astrum in it, which is the star properly; that which is sensible and that which is insensible has a sydereall spirit or a sydereall body in it. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “the invisible Astrum or Sydereall Spirit in the Stars we cannot see; so that not the body of the Sun, but the Spirit in the body, is the Sun properly” [CH1.34]
    • “That which is sensible and that which is insensible hath a sydereall spirit or a sydereall body in it” [N120]
  2. In man: the invisible man from the Firmament, the life that dwells in the body and is properly the man; mortal, distinct from the immortal soul breathed in by God; fed from the Firmament, it draws all arts and human wisdom from the stars, leads the body where it imagines, and stirs each man’s dexterity. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “which life is the Syderiall Spirit, and is properly the Man, not flesh and blood” [MICRO.23]
    • “As the Syderiall Spirit is the life of the Body, so the Spirit of the Lord is the life of the Intellectuall Soule” [MICRO.23]
    • “the Syderiall spirit of Man or the invisible Man (which is the In-mate of the body) hath its food from the externall Aire and Fire or Firmament” [CH1.40]
    • “one a syderiall Spirit from the Firmament, the other from the breath of life, which is the Intellectuall Soul inspired from God” [MICRO.14]
    • “Man hath three parts, a mortall Body with a Syderiall Spirit, and an immortall Soule” [MICRO.14]
    • “that Astrall body or Syderiall Spirit, which is the true Astrall Man” [MICRO.27]
    • “This Spirit is mortall” [MICRO.27]
    • “And as the Sydereall Spirit dwells in the Body and works therein day and night” [MICRO.24]
    • “the syderiall spirit of Man attracteth all Arts, sciences, and faculties, and all humane Wisdome from the Rayes or beams of the superiour Stars or constellations” [CH1.41]
    • “Such is the force of the Sydereall spirit upon the body, that whithersoever it imagineth and dreameth it carryeth and leadeth the very body up and down with it” [CH5.76]
    • “every man according to his dexterity which the sydereall spirit causeth and exciteth is the forger of his own fortune” [CH6.44]
  3. What rules the Old man in most of those whom titles, degrees and names make proud, named where there is said to be no life of Christ and no holy Spirit. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “who suffer the Old man to be ruled by the Sydereall Spirit” [CH3.46]
  4. That from which all natural things may be learned, after the stars are said to pierce into man. Reading 1: man’s own sydereall spirit, which comes from the Firmament. Basis: MICRO.14 and CH1.41 give man a syderiall spirit from the Firmament that draws wisdom from the stars. Reading 2: the Firmament’s own spirit. Basis: the words “of the Firmament”. (veiled · several · voice: CROLL)
    • “so that of the syderiall spirit of the Firmament we may learn all Naturall things” [MICRO.18]

Sign

The book spells it: signe, signes, Signes

  1. An outward thing that points to a hidden thing signified and is less than it: herbs are only a sign of the Word. (veiled · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “hearbs are not the medicines, but a signe onely of the Word signified” [CH2.13]
    • “Grace exceedeth Nature, and the thing signified excelleth the signe” [N149]
  2. A sign in heaven: every living creature has its ascendant sign there, and men are born in a happy sign. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “every living Creature hath its Ascendant signe in Heaven” [N125]
    • “who were born in a happy signe” [CH6.45]
  3. An outward token of a disease, not the disease itself. (plain · Stated · voice: CROLL)
    • “those Accidents and Excrements are but the signes of the disease, and not the disease it selfe” [CH4.6]
  4. Signes named with Characters, by which true magicians effect great things, and the Terrestiall signe to which the Gamahaea is a wedlock. Interpreted (my inference): an earthly sign joined by influence to the Superiors. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “by a Word written and Characters or Signes” [CH2.16]
    • “the Gamahaea of Influentiall Wedlock to the Terrestiall signe” [CH2.21]

Signatures

The book spells it: Signatures

  1. Something like things and the diseases they cure have alike, named beside qualities and roots as a reason why like things cure. Interpreted: the narrowest reading of this one place. (plain · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
    • “Like things are said to be the Remedies of diseases because they are of the same Anatomy of Nature, and because they have the like Signatures, Qualities and Roots” [CH4.6]
  2. Outward marks by which the kind of a man is known, as whether he is a dog, fox or wolf, or as sloth and noise mark the drones. Interpreted from the two passages. (veiled · Interpreted · voice: CROLL)
